Dear Minister, According to the draft law, the law is complemented by a regulation allowing the State itself to carry out the preparatory activities for building permits for the construction of the sea for the development of sea wind parks and aquaculture and to submit these building permits to a public competition. The drafters have explained that the interest of the State is not to restrict requirements for economic operators or their possibilities, but that the State must have the possibility to decide on the economic activities of specific areas. According to the draft, the national representative body will submit applications for a building permit for [the] sea wind parks to be built in the sea areas. According to the explanatory memorandum, this body could also be the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications. There is therefore a conflict of interest in the draft: the body responsible for managing the energy markets should not be responsible for developing sea wind parks. What do you think of that?
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U3	Thank you. On the contrary, I think that there are situations where it is necessary for the state to take the preparatory action itself in order to speed up the processes. And sometimes there may be a situation where the developer has not taken action at enough speed to prepare the planned area for a wind farm building permit. Then it makes sense for the country to intervene and carry it out. The aim of all this activity is still to enable us to build sea wind parks as quickly as possible. And, of course, not only wind farms, but also the part concerning aquaculture.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U29	Okay, now we're on item 19. This is the first reading of the draft law amending the Law on Safety at Sea, initiated by the Government of the Republic. I ask the Minister of Economy and Infrastructure, Taavi Aasa, to present the speech chair of the Riigikogu.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U30	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! I will introduce the draft law supplementing the Maritime Safety Act. The amendment relates directly to the readings of next year's state budget here in the Riigikogu. The bill complements the Maritime Safety Act with the provision that ship-owners will have to pay the waterway charge by 25% less than the threshold laid down in the same law in 2022. The draft concerns ship-owners and the Transport Board, who keep records of and collects waterway charges. As a result of the COVID-19 pandemic, ship owners found themselves in a difficult situation as passenger and freight transport was significantly reduced as a result of border closures. Most countries took different measures to alleviate the situation. From 1 April 2020 until 31 March 2021, ship owners in Estonia were exempted from the payment of the waterway charge. From 1 April of this year until the end of the year, a reduced rate of 50% shall apply. As a result of the exemption and subsequent reductions, the freight volumes of Estonian ports increased, while the freight volumes of ports in neighbouring countries decreased. This positive trend has continued this year. The continued reduction in the amount of the bill will have a positive impact on shipowners, ports, transport and forwarding companies and increase the competitiveness of freight corridors passing through Estonia and Estonian exports. A 25% reduction in the cost of waterway charges in 2022 will bring an estimated cost to the state budget of around 4.5 million, but the positive impact may amount to approximately 16 million euros if freight volumes are added. Honourable members of the Riigikogu, I ask you to support the draft.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U44	Good morning again. It'll be early in the morning. This draft law amending the Law on Safety at Sea 455 was initiated by the Government on 29 September and the Riigikogu's Board of Directors was adopted on 30 September this year. At the meeting of the Economic Commission, Minister Taavi Aas presented the draft on 12 October. The main content of the bill in 2022 is the determination of the waterway charge 25% lower than that laid down in the same law in order to motivate consignors to channel trade flows through Estonian ports. Waterway charges will be reduced on all ships entering Estonian ports and port raids. Kaido Rosin said that, according to the distinction valid until the end of this year, there are also incentives for port visits. Rosin has examined whether it is not currently intended for this purpose and how it relates specifically to the benefit of the following year. Hana-Maarja Helinurm, Legal Adviser to the Maritime Economy Department of the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Communications, replied that the number of visits had been reduced due to the fact that, until the end of March of this year, the vessels had been fully exempted from the payment of waterway charges. As the 25% reduction is foreseen for the whole calendar year, the current logic of the payment of the waterway charges continues according to the law, that is to say, the number of visits remains the same. The letter sent by the Union of shipowners to several parties, including the Economic Commission, on 14 October also indirectly concerns this draft with a request to continue the passenger shipping support measure for at least 2022 and 2023. This concerned the quarterly support paid to a passenger company engaged in international voyages, the so-called tax refund. This letter will be discussed before the second reading. But the Commission made decisions. Firstly, to propose that the draft be put on the plenary agenda on 27 October, which became smoothly today, 28 October; secondly, to propose that the first reading be stopped and third reading. propose five working days for the amendments, i.e. 5.15 p.m. on 4 November 2021. All the decisions were taken in consensus and were taken by Riho Breivel, Jüri Jaanson, Raimond Kaljulaid, Mikhail Korb, Kristen Michal, Sven Sester, Mikhail Stalnukhin and the speaker here. ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U46	Still eight. Good manager! Ladies and gentlemen, Good minister! First of all, I want to congratulate the Minister. If I made a bit of a pickle with him on the previous draft, in the case of this bill, I must sincerely say that the Minister for Economic Affairs and Communications, as much as we have had the honour of cooperating, has always tried the best on shipping. But as he said in answering my question, nor in the discussions of the Ministry of Finance on how the cost-benefit side is calculated, it is very well and precisely understood. As an economic measure related to the spread of the Corona virus, in the previous coalition, we jointly adopted the Act amending the laws relating to the Estonian State's additional budget for 2020 law, which exempted all ships entering Estonian ports and the raid of the port from payment of the waterway charge from 1 April of the year until the end of this year, and the state budget for that year is 50% of the waterway charges. This decision has had a positive impact on Estonia's competitiveness. Compared to 2019 and 2020, Estonia gained additional freight volumes, thus additional revenue for the state. While the volume of freight in our Baltic Sea region decreased by 7.9% at that time, the volume of goods transported by the Port of Tallinn increased by 7%, i.e. 1.4 million tonnes. If this decision had not been taken, this figure would not have increased by 7% but would have decreased by 8%, i.e. it would have been even lower. PricewaterhouseCoopers has for many years analysed the economic impact of the Estonian freight corridor and, based on these studies, it is estimated that this increase in freight volume will have a positive impact on the state budget, approximately 16 million euros. This will cover most of the cost of the exemption measure. It is important to take into account that if such a decision had not been taken, Estonia would have lost heavily in the volume of freight transport and also in the revenue of the state as compared to what was planned. Business forecasts show that, without this measure, a large part of the revenue from the planned waterway charges would have been lost and that freight volumes would have been significantly reduced. As a result, the revenue from the state budget would have been more than EUR 35 million lower. But now they want to increase the fee twice compared to this year next year. Is there now a desire to discourage entrepreneurship and to give up the revenue of the state? It is our party's view that, in the light of the above, budget planning should be continued at a rate of 50% from 1 January next year. I remember that, along with the Minister for Economic Affairs and Communications, improvements were made to the state budget this year as chairman of the Committee on Monetary Affairs, with which we kept the waterway charges low, which brought cash flows and cargo ships to our ports. The representative of the Commission also spoke about Estonian passenger shipping. Estonian passenger shipping is a well-known trademark of Estonia and a strong guarantor of the free movement of international travellers and goods. The passenger shipping support measure introduced by Regulation No 57 of the Minister of Economic Affairs and Infrastructure of 24 September last year, "Conditions and arrangements for supporting international passenger shipping', helped to maintain passenger shipping between neighbouring countries even if the number of passengers was minimal and the organisation of journeys to shipowners was loss-making. This measure was able to maintain a large number of jobs and avoid a catastrophic situation for Estonian maritime workers. Given the current increase in the spread of the COVID-19 virus in Estonia and neighbouring countries, a serious question arises: what has changed that the Government of the Republic has decided not to continue this aid measure? According to experts, it is only possible to restore passenger flow to pre-pandemic levels in 20252026. By 31 December, the Commission shall submit a report to the European Parliament and to the Council on the implementation of this Decision. This development is devastating for the Estonian maritime industry, as a large part of Estonia's professional crew would become unemployed. If we want Estonian passenger shipping to be sustainable in the future, we should also support passenger shipping next year. Thank you.

These internal borders vary between 25 and 100 euros, with extremely high levels in one municipality. They are very different in local governments, which is reasonable and which has been purposeful, because local circumstances are different and need to be taken into account. But these local circumstances are changing and therefore these thresholds need to be adjusted slightly more frequently. Perhaps today is a good time when they can be readjusted, because people's needs and circumstances give them greater coercion. I fully agree that the subsistence limit for 10500 households [who receive income support] is low. There are 162 households living in relative poverty in Estonia, and a very roughly one third of them are those who suffer from the price of electricity and the price of gas. I am very pleased to say that the Government of the Republic is coming up with a proposal to mitigate the rise in electricity and gas prices for households living in relative poverty from CO2 resources. Why is this aspect of hearing a child very important? We have a valid Code of Civil Procedure which imposes on the judge, as it has been for many years, the obligation to listen to the child on matters relating to him. Similarly, in § 21 of the Child Protection Act, we have said that the obligation to listen to a child is in all matters relating to his or her life. Obviously, the disputes we are trying to resolve in the conciliation process before the courts, where parents are discussing the sensible arrangements for meeting the child, the size of the child's livelihood, the issues of the change in custody, are very much affecting the child's life, and that is where the child's opinion is also important. We also know that identifying a child's opinion is an exercise that requires competence and skill. We have children who are young and cannot speak, we have children who have mental disabilities or mental disorders, we have children who are victims of abuse, eyewitnesses of domestic violence, and all these situations require specific competence. I do not think that it is too much to ask that those conciliators who have obtained the invitation be able to pass through this additional training module so that, in the same way as the other conciliators whom we are training by the state today, but not only those who are not members of the conciliators' association and who have received the invitation and are going through this child's hearing training to provide the service.

Yes, the government association has tried to put responsibility on the local authorities, but that is the responsibility of the state and the government, and this should continue to be fulfilled in the future. We could actually have the same consensus that we are not cutting at the expense of children and young people. Or if the country is really going bankrupt, that cuts must be made, then children and young people and education will be the last place in which we are stalling the costs, ladies and gentlemen. One more thing. In fact, engaging in recreational activities, engaging in sports, gives our young people a positive experience, helps to inject joy and success into them. It is not too late for this very embarrassing and shameful cut [to stop] the last one in life. Today we have the opportunity to vote against the Commission's proposal so that this draft will remain in the process and we will find this money together next year. We support children, we support recreational and sporting activities! Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U241	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, Lately, there has really been talk here, and it has caused a lot of emotions about additional funding for recreational activities. My argument is that the current additional support for interest activities is not a systematic activity. Its purpose was to create new activities, to enable young people who were underprivileged to be interested, to support associations of interest and to provide area support. It's not bad goals. However, the basis for granting the aid is, in my opinion, completely wrong. Every municipality has its own face, depending on traditions, support systems and existing service provider. The previous speaker was right about the amount of extra money. Today's difference is 4 million. According to the Ministry of Finance, the total cost of the activities in the municipalities is 160 million. In my opinion, it is very difficult for the state to support specific activities in very diverse municipalities at an optimal and equal level. Local life should still be organised by the local government. The local government's concern, I stress, is not a lack of money, but a lack of specific areas, such as supervisors. The municipality cannot ask for additional support for the activities of interest itself. The subsidy is allocated on the basis of public statistics, i.e. the less the municipality itself has contributed to the development of the social and interest field, the greater the support. This support must not be used to finance existing activities of interest, but only to create new opportunities. Here is an example of where I live. I'm from Keila City. The city of Keila has committed 1.4 million euros in its budget to support interest activities, and that is not all. The financing of the city of Keila has been done differently. All the bases in Keila City I emphasise: all the bases in Keila City where the activities are carried out are free of charge for users. The additional support for the City of Keila's activities in 2021 was more than EUR 23000. In comparison with the neighbouring municipality, where the number of young people is virtually the same, the amount is over EUR 345000. The most absurd of all in this situation is that 150 children from neighbouring municipalities participate in the activities offered by the city of Keila, but they are not willing to do so, even partially. The town of Võru is in exactly the same situation. At national level, support should be given to the sector with a broader view and include the provision of regional services among the award criteria. The aim must be to create a level playing field for children and young people involved in activities of interest. Currently, thousands of young people participate in activities of interest in neighbouring municipalities. Municipalities are not obliged to provide services to "foreign" children and there is no obligation to ensure that their children who go to other municipalities are able to engage in activities. Here, national regional support aimed at those municipalities that are engaged in cross-border activities for children of their neighbours could help. We would support those who have invested themselves in an interest base and created opportunities for action. This support would be based on a child-based and electronic database. This topic is closely linked to educational migration, as most children who go away from home in school also take advantage of the opportunities of recreational activities close to this school. The purpose of state support is to ensure that all children and young people in Estonia have access to recreational activities regardless of the place where they are registered. I would argue that additional support for recreational activities was created when we had very small municipalities that could not provide all the services to citizens. Now, after the administrative reform, all municipalities should be able to manage their own lives. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, the local elections have just ended and there will be a budget for 2022. The country has had a difficult time in the crisis and has had to support many different sectors. Sh has been supported by local governments with two additional budgets of around 170 million. In addition, due to the rapid economic recovery, the local government revenue base will grow by around 120 million in 2022. Four million were taken away from the activities of interest. My argument is that no children will be deprived of anything, the local governments' income base is sufficient and the state aid has been solid in the crisis. This means that new councils would be able to provide at least 2021 level in the 2022 budget. In fact, most probably do even more, such as Rae, Keila or Elva.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U249	Ladies and gentlemen, Today we are discussing an extremely important law that will bring our working relations back into civilised social order. The organisation of a society where labour market decisions are made in negotiations between entrepreneurs and workers, capital and work, where, in fact, those who are involved in the labour market and who are concerned by the regulations in question agree among themselves on the conditions of pay, working conditions, all possible conditions, and the possibility of extending this agreement to the whole sector. This means that an employee is given a guarantee that every employee will be treated equally, whatever his or her native language, whether he or she is a man or a woman, in his or her passport. Among other things, it helps to combat the pay gap very clearly, to combat the lure of workers from other countries who have lower prices in their home country into this slave labour. All these problems will be countered by an extended collective agreement, which we are now making possible once again. And let us correct the error that the Supreme Court indicated that the current regulation did not provide for the principle of representativeness. Today, we have corrected this mistake. Today, we can say that when workers' organisations and trade unions come together in a broad way, they agree on working conditions, it applies to everyone, whether or not you are a member of this organisation. It also means that, in fact, we are embracing the Constitution, we are embracing what the Constitution provides for, saying that workers' organisations and business organisations are very specific collectives, which the Constitution must protect. With this we defend our constitution and say: join us! By joining, we will do better Estonia together, with better and fairer working relations. The same applies to these smaller points, such as those of the trustees. We guarantee that if there are actually several trustees in the company, they will have time to do their job, they will actually be able to represent employees' concerns and ideas, and will therefore be a better partner for an entrepreneur who does not see what the company looks like. It is the trustee who guarantees this vision and thus guarantees more effective work and effective action, better productivity. Similarly, the civilised world also includes unemployment benefits, differences in the payment of supplementary unemployment benefits, additional unemployment insurance, which says that work is your right. You don't have to be in a situation where you accept any working conditions as long as you don't starve to death. That is why we are watching that the unemployed also have their rights, so that the unemployed can also prepare themselves better for the next job and that they can actually talk on equal terms about working conditions. So I hope you all support this bill. This benefits both the employee and the entrepreneur. It is good for all of us because it says: we are a real country, we really care about our people and their rights. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U254	Our fourth item is the first reading of the statement by the members of the Riigikogu, Peeter Ernitsa, Jaak Juske, Reuben Kaalepi, Uno Kaskpeiti, Martin Helme, Riho Breiveli, Leo Kunnase, Merry Aarti, Siim Pohlaku, Anti Polandmets, Helle-Moonika Helme, Mart Helme, Henn Põlluaasa, Rene Koka, Alar Lanemani, Kert Kingo, Paul Puustusmaa, Jaak Valge, Tarmo Kruusimäe, Urmas Reitelmann and Kalle Grünthal in support of Russia's indigenous peoples. Please waitress Ruuben Kaalepi, member of the Riigikogu. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U255	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Good Riigikogu! The draft statement was initiated by 21 members of the Riigikogu from three political groups to draw attention to one of the most important issues of Estonia's value-based foreign policy concerning the fate of our tribal peoples and other indigenous peoples under the rule of the Russian Federation. This draft is the result of a long journey that began already during the previous government's term of office, when the foreign policy development plan for 2030 was adopted and laid down and formulated the objectives and principles of Estonian foreign policy. This is one way in which Estonia can draw attention and exert international pressure to stop the severe repression of the indigenous peoples of Russia, including our relatives. This draft draws the public's attention to the Russian Federation's decades of Russification Policy towards many of Russia's indigenous peoples. This policy of Russification has been particularly intensified in recent years. It is no secret that every year the situation of indigenous peoples and the pressure of Russification have become ever more brutal. Schools are closed and education in their own language is prevented. Freedom of religion is restricted, natural resources are abducted. There are dozens and hundreds of concrete examples of legislative obstacles in Russia to the learning and teaching of national languages in educational institutions. The common pattern of behaviour of the Russian authorities is the repression of peaceful demonstrations in support of the rights of indigenous peoples by means of repression and physical force, the declaration and closure of civil movements defending the interests of indigenous peoples, the repression, imprisonment and unjustly placing in a psychiatric hospital of activists who stand up for the rights of indigenous peoples. A very important part of the Russification process is to allow indigenous peoples to lose their education in their own language. This has been particularly intensified since 2018 when amendments to the educational law of the Russian Federation were adopted, which prevent educational institutions from learning and teaching national languages. There is no need to be a psychic to see its outcome over decades. If the language is destroyed, the nation will be destroyed. Against this particular amendment of the law, demonstrations by indigenous peoples took place in many parts of Russia in order to protect education in their own language. These peaceful demonstrations have been obstructed and suppressed by the internal security forces inherent in Russia. For example, demonstrations organised by the authorities in 2017 and 2018 to protect education in their own language were launched several times in Kalmõkkia, Elistas, Bashkortostan and Tatarstan. Ingušia's mass protestors fell victim to persecution by the authorities in the same year, 2018. As if not enough, the next typical pattern of Russian Federation behaviour completely ignores the right of indigenous peoples to benefit from the use of natural resources in their native habitats, as stated in the United Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples. This right is not respected and has never been respected by the Russian authorities. The destruction of natural resources and sacred places is often encouraged. For example, the lack of action by the authorities in 2018 resulted in the widespread destruction of the Burjaatia forests. It should not be forgotten here that indigenous peoples' habitats are among the most resource-rich areas in Russia, which are consistently exploited by the Russian authorities and energy companies, because Russia's geostrategic power depends on it. And this is where the countries of Europe that consume these resources are also jointly responsible for all forms of repression of indigenous peoples. For example, natural gas extracted from Nenetsland is also used by Estonia. The construction of the Ust-Luga or Laugasuu harbour complex and city in the immediate vicinity of Estonia has caused irreversible damage to the Votian and Insur folk fragments almost permanently dying out. It was in this area that the last Votian and Izuri villages were located until recently. These Laugasuu villages were nothing less than a living museum, where the ancient language, which was an invaluable subject for the linguists, was still sounded. These villages were sacrificed for oil transit, for which huge industrial facilities complexes were erected and thousands of workers were brought in. The destruction of these last villages by the country, which has been the harsh ruler of the Votian and Insimians for centuries, is like trampling on historical memories, the winner's laughter over what he won. Protecting the rights of indigenous peoples in Russia is very difficult. All the associations that stand for this will inevitably fall victim to repression. This is an increasing restriction of freedom of expression, freedom of religion and civil rights. Russia has a direct ban on the creation of parties aimed at upholding the rights of indigenous peoples. The following organisations of indigenous peoples have been banned or declared extremist since 2006: the Burjad National Congress, the Tatari Public Centre, the Tatari Youth Association Azatliq, the Chuvash National and Cultural Rebirth Society, the Nuori Karelia Youth Association and the Kalmõki Repressed Peoples Foundation. The section of extremism is also used in Russia to persecute the origins. The most under attack has been the Mari Nativeism, which has been practiced by the authorities as a direct hindrance. The works of one of the best-known native priests, or Kartti Vitali Tanakov, have been declared extremist and have thus banned several religious texts that are important to Mari's originality, and in our sense, scriptures. Dozens and dozens of fighters for the rights of indigenous peoples have been hit by repression in Russia. Many of them have been imprisoned. It would be impossible to list all of them here, so let's just say a few character names. Vladimir Hamutayev, scientist and Chairman of the Negado National Movement, was forced to leave their homeland in 2013. Fauzia Bairamova, chairman of the Tatar People's Council, imprisoned in 2014. Rafis Kashapov, leader of the Tatar National Movement, imprisoned in 2015. Bulat Sagina, Chairman of Burjad Soel, Burjad Association, imprisoned 2015. Ruslan Gvašev and Shamsudin Neguch, Cherkess nationalists, imprisoned in 2017. Ojub Titiyev, Chechen human rights activist, imprisoned 2018. Angela Matijeva and Zafira Sautijeva, spokespersons for the mass protests of the enguish, recounted in 2018. Batyrhan Agzamov, member of the Tatar Youth Association Azatliq, repressed 2019. Fayeel Alsynov, head of the Bashkor Community, repressed 2019. Airat Dilmuhamedov, Bashkir's activist, imprisoned in 2019. Actors of Kalmõk Batyr Boromannayev, Vladimir Dovdanov, Badma Bjurchiyev and Chairman of the Repressed Peoples' Foundation Arkadi Gorjayev, imprisoned all together in 2019. Aleksandr Gabychev, Yakut shaman, imprisoned and placed in a psychiatric hospital in 2020. But it's not just that. Very often, those who speak for indigenous peoples in Russia have to fear not only for the loss of liberty but also for their own lives. The murders and kidnappings of indigenous activists are also common in Russia, which the authorities refuse to investigate correctly. Among the most important are the murder of Aslan Žukov, the Cherkess youth activist Adõgee Khas in 2010, the murder of Muslim Hashagulgov, the engullish nationalist in 2018, the attempted murder of Yevgeny Hamaganov, the founder of the Burjaatian human rights movement Erhe 2015 and the kidnapping of Rustam Lianov, the enguchi activist in 2018. Dear Riigikogu! It would be impossible to imagine that all indigenous peoples would endure these repressions, this brutal violence and this repression in silence. It is customary for dramatic repressive steps to provoke only more desperate resistance. But for the vast majority of indigenous peoples, this resistance has been heroicly nonviolent. But the situation is desperate, and it has also led to desperate steps. Albert Razin was a renowned scientist, sociologist, and legendary social figure in Udmurtia. He had been one of the first Udmurt national congress convened in 1991. In 2018, he signed a public letter, one of the many, protesting against amendments to the law restricting learning in his own language. The authorities ignored all protests. On September 10th, 2019 at 8:30, Razin Udmurt's Republic duuma came with two companions to the pict, where he kept the Russian-speaking posters, one of which had Mr Rasul Hamzatov's poetry "And when my language disappears tomorrow, I am ready to die today" and the second question, "Do I have a homeland?'. At 9:45 a.m., he spilled gas and lit him on fire. Albert Razin died in the hospital from severe burns without regaining consciousness the same night. At least at present, the Republic of Estonia does not have the physical means to protect our Russian relatives and all hundreds of their tribes of destiny from forced extinction. It is for those who are forgetting their mother tongue and whose population is declining every year that the greatest need is a national impulse, and even the tiny knowledge that they are also important to the world. If Finno-Ugric peoples disappear, we Finns and perhaps Hungarians will be the last to keep this heritage. Honourable Riigikogu! The Russian people, who have historically inhabited the western part of the country, cannot regard themselves as the sole host of the vast territory that is subject to the entire Moscow Empire. This statement supports the undying right of self-determination of all nations. The same principle as the Estonian state and our manifesto of independence have been established. This statement also emphasises the need to support the preservation of the indigenous peoples of Russia and to present their history, culture and situation throughout the world. The statement calls on the United Nations, the European Union and their Member States to recognise the unmistakable right of the indigenous peoples of Russia to preserve their national character, language and culture without any discrimination or persecution. Russia has been a member of the Council of Europe since 1996 and the brutal repression of freedoms and rights in that country cannot be acceptable to other Member States. The activities of the Russian Federation towards indigenous peoples are also in clear contradiction with the Declaration of the Rights of Indigenous Peoples adopted by the United Nations General Assembly on 13 September 2007. This statement also confirms the need to examine whether the activities of the Russian Federation comply on 9 December 1948 with the definition of genocide in the second article of the United Nations Convention on the Prevention and Punishment of Genocide, according to which genocide is to be regarded as, inter alia, a national, ethnic, racial or religious entity intentionally imposing conditions of residence which lead to the total or partial destruction of the entity. It must be acknowledged that the Russification policy is implemented with the obvious intention of losing the ethnic, ethnic and indeed, in some cases religious peculiarities of indigenous peoples. Estonia cannot remain indifferent, being a member of the European Union, the Council of Europe, the OECD and the OSCE and still being a member of the UN Security Council. But more so, the Estonians and the Estonian state, as a free Finno-Ugric nation state, have a moral obligation to support our brothers and other indigenous peoples living in Russia, whose way of life, languages, culture, customs and religions are threatened by the Russian policy of our eastern neighbour. We are not standing up for Russian minorities simply because of opposition. We do this not only because it is persecuted minorities, but most of all because we are talking about indigenous peoples who have lived in their country for hundreds or even thousands of years and whose attempt, in spite of centuries of foreign power, to remain themselves deserves the greatest respect and recognition. On 26 May 2020, the President of the Riigikogu received a referral and a request for assistance, signed by Mr Fauzia Bairamova, President of the National Assembly of Tatari, Syreś Boläeń, Head of the Kalmük Repressed Peoples Foundation, Arkadi Gorjayev, representative of the Kuban National Movement, Dmytro Dorovskikh, co-founder of the freedom movement in Idel-Uural, Rafis Kashapov, and Vladimir Hamutayev, Chairman of the Burjad National Congress. In this address, the Riigikogu was asked to condemn the policy of Russification, the restriction of the sovereignty of the national republics within the Russian Federation, to demand the release of political prisoners and to support the efforts of indigenous peoples to defend their culture, identity, languages and tradition. It was a request for help at a high level, which represented not only the signatories' nationalities but all the repressed indigenous peoples of Russia. At national level, Estonia has completely a soft force and we are constantly talking about its implementation in defending its value area, including towards Russia. It would be fair and justified to link it to a clear long-term strategy based on our national values and helping to achieve our national interests. We can only respond to Russia's so-called compatriotic policy by supporting and encouraging the cultural self-determination of Finno-Ugric peoples. During the discussion of this draft in the Committee on Foreign Affairs of the Riigikogu, Members expressed doubts as to whether a value-based foreign policy could not undermine our relations with Russia. I think it is our responsibility to conduct a value-based foreign policy. We have been chosen here by the Estonian people to stand up for the principles on which the Estonian state has historically been built. In fact, the Committee on Foreign Affairs recommended that this statement be withdrawn and that it be hoped that new steps will be taken by the government at international level to support the indigenous peoples of Russia or that this issue will be raised in the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe. I am not so naive as to hope for a value-based foreign policy in a situation where the government has trampled it all over the world, from Taiwan to Mali. The Riigikogu must take the initiative here. I would like to stress that, on several occasions in the Committee on Foreign Affairs, we, by the initiators of the draft, proposed amendments and improvements by all the political groups. We also took an extension and were ready to listen to all thoughts and opinions with the desire to find a consensus that would be ready to be signed by all political parties. No proposals were received. Ladies and gentlemen, As a Finno-Ugric nation state, Estonia has a moral obligation to support those nations with a long and dignified history that are threatened with a direct extinction as a result of the Russification Policy. If Estonia does not take a step towards this, we will no longer be able to talk about a value-based foreign policy. Unfortunately, there are those who think that Estonia should not have an independent foreign policy, because it would violate relations with Russia and show us too stubbornly in the eyes of the West. This attitude must not continue to sound in this Chamber of the Riigikogu because it would be sad and shameful. I ask all members of the Riigikogu, regardless of the political group, regardless of the world view, to support this draft. We stand with the support of repressed indigenous peoples. This is a matter of our national dignity. Thank you. I'm ready to answer questions.

If this bill were voted down, it would be a huge blow to the credibility of our political class and our government. It would signal the stagnation and bureaucracy of our foreign policy.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U281	Ladies and gentlemen, The Committee on Foreign Affairs unexpectedly asked me to present the Commission's position on this statement. Initially, Mikhail Lotman, vice-president of the Commission, wanted to do so, but unfortunately he is ill. I am about to say that I am going to mix a little content with procedural issues; formally, I should only talk about the procedure. First of all, I am very grateful to Mr Kaalep that this subject has reached this hall. This is indeed an important issue and is also evidenced by the fact that the Committee on Foreign Affairs discussed the text of this statement in as many as seven sessions. I believe that even the Treaty of Accession to the European Union or the issues of Tartu Peace have not been discussed in so many meetings of the Commission. This application was submitted by 21 members of the Riigikogu on 12 May. The deadline for submitting amendments was 27 May, unfortunately no amendments were received by that time. In some respects, the Commission was forced to take a decision, although the authors repeatedly pointed out that they were prepared to change the wording. Through procedural amendments, this text could no longer be amended, and the Commission could not, as a result of the opposition of the political groups, manage to produce a new text that would be better suited to the other political groups. As a result, this text is also, more or less in its original form, in this vote today, with some linguistic corrections that do not change the content. We started this procedure in the Committee on Foreign Affairs on 14 September. Ruuben Kaalep introduced the content and purpose of the draft and emphasised, as in this Chamber, that this is an important and serious issue concerning our eastern policies and those aspects in which Estonia has a special and important role to play, in order to protect and at international level, to draw attention to the rights and problems of our culturally connected relatives and all other indigenous peoples. I will say immediately that this is a position that all the other Members of the Commission also agreed unanimously. Mikhail Lotman said he understood the spirit of such a statement, but its formulation in relation to indigenous peoples is problematic. Ruuben Kaalep considered Finno-Ugric people the most important, but the problem concerns all the indigenous peoples of Russia, as Kaalep said. Lotman pointed out that the Finno-Ugric peoples were not mentioned at all in the text of the statement, and that the term "indigenous peoples" should be replaced by the term "denial peoples." The chairman of the committee proposed that the political groups should shape their opinion and that the debate in the Committee on Foreign Affairs continue. It continued on 16 September, but no substantive discussion was reached this time. The committee president, Marko Mihkelson, proposed that the contents of the draft be more serious and discussed and asked the initiators for agreement to postpone the decision to put the draft on the plenary agenda. The initiators were represented by Henn Põlluaas, who agreed to this proposal. On 30 September, the first major debate took place, with various experts and representatives of the indigenous peoples of Russia invited. Sven-Erik Soosaar, expert member of the United Nations Permanent Forum on Indigenous Peoples, Jaak Prozes, adviser to the Fenno-Ugria Foundation, representative of Ingerizomians from Thailando-Kalevi Raudalainen and, in addition, adviser to the International Organisations and Human Rights Office of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Rein Oidekivi, were invited. I'm going to open up a little bit of what one or another expert said, because it's essential for further understanding of the current situation. Soosaar explained that Russia recognises the existence of indigenous peoples, but they have a list of small indigenous peoples under federal law, with a maximum of 50,000 people. Right now, there are 48 indigenous peoples on this list. The main programmes aimed at protecting indigenous peoples are specifically aimed at these small indigenous peoples, and this means that most of the large Finno-Ugric peoples are out of protection, the so-called defence programmes of the Russian Federation. Prozes said the same thing and said that what concerns the restriction of freedom of religion for Maris is not factually true. He added that, as far as demonstrations are concerned, Russia has a law restricting the organisation of demonstrations and that it applies everywhere and to everyone. From a Finno-Ugric point of view, it has not been heard that ethnic demonstrations have been disbanded. Prozes proposed trying to find common positions with, for example, Hungary or Finland or raising the issue at EU level, as the problem also concerns language groups other than Finno-Ugric people. Raudalainen explained that Russia has a much more fragmented term for indigenous peoples than it is internationally understood. There is a certain territoriality attached to the activities of small nations and they are forced to cooperate with local municipal authorities. Raudalainen was of the opinion that, in the current wording, the statement could cause more damage than benefits. He felt that the text of the statement should be more clearly focused. Oidekivi of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs said that the topic of indigenous peoples is one of the highest priorities among the human rights issues of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The United Nations General Assembly has announced the International Decade of Indigenous Languages 2022 to 2032. According to Oidekivi, the adoption of the statement in the Riigikogu would not help to improve the situation of indigenous peoples, but rather may fear the opposite reaction from the Russian authorities. He recommended consulting people who are in direct contact with Finno-Ugric peoples, as well as representatives of Setos. Maria Jufereva-Skuratovski said that indigenous peoples should be protected wisely so that they would not be harmed more, and asked whether Finno-Ugric representatives were also represented in local governments and what about the Finno-Ugric people's representation in the Russian authorities. Prozes replied that none of the Finno-Ugric republics in Russia were run by a representative of the indigenous people. Lotman said that all the peoples of Russia had been discriminated against and believed that the statement should not be an isolated act, but must be consistent. The Commission then discussed this issue on 11 October. The members of the committee expressed doubts about the adoption of the statement in this form in the Riigikogu and were of the opinion that it would be more effective to raise the issue of the situation of indigenous peoples living in Russia, together with co-ordinate countries at international level. One way would be to launch a debate in the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe, for example. At that point it was clear that no member of the Commission or political group, with the exception of the initiators, would support the adoption of the text of the statement in the proposed form. The chairman of the committee, Marko Mihkelson, recommended that, following discussions, the initiators withdraw the text and consider reaching a consensus on the issue at large and a broader discussion of the matter in the Riigikogu. We have not reached a consensus today, but at least we are discussing it more widely here. Ruuben Kaalep, the initiators' representative, did not agree with the proposal to withdraw the text, but agreed to change the wording of the text of the statement if necessary. On 12 October, the following meeting was held at which the chairman of the committee proposed that another substantive discussion be held with experts. This took place on 18 October, where the Commission heard the views of Aare Hõrn, Foreign Affairs Advisor to the Union of Northwest and Setomaa, representative of the NGO URALIC Centre. Northwest gave a thorough and quite realistic overview of the situation of minorities in Russia and Russia's national policies. Importantly, Northwest said: "Because the statement begins with the concepts of genocide and deliberate Russification, he does not consider this to be a realistic assessment of the situation. " He did not see any reference to the Genocide in the original address addressed to the President of the Riigikogu. He added that international human rights organisations, which closely monitor the situation of the indigenous peoples of Russia, are critical of Russia, but they do not use such concepts. He saw no reason why Estonia should be tougher in its statement. Northwest was of the opinion that the term "genocide" should not be handled lightly, which would reduce Estonia's international credibility. Northwest was of the opinion that such a statement could have a negative effect on our tribal peoples in Russia. The statement would provide Russia with good material for an anti-Estonian information war, would make it difficult to communicate apolitically, and cooperation between Estonia and civil society with Russian tribal peoples at grassroots level. At the same time, Northwest said that it was difficult to see the benefits this statement could bring. For its part, the Northwest suggested not to accept this statement. Raivo Tamm, who is also the president of the Finno-Ugric Support Group of the Riigikogu, stated that this was a difficult situation and felt that the Commission could also listen to the views of representatives of the tribal peoples. Mr Lotman was of the opinion that this statement was not good in this form, and he was not in favour of using the word 'genocide' either. Imre Sooäär asked whether, in conclusion to today's debate, the statement would rather damage the current situation of our tribal peoples. Northwest said the statement would put the activists and leaders of our tribal movement under attack in Russia. The Treasure of the Gentle agreed with the Northwest. Henn Põljaas said that the situation of small and indigenous peoples in Russia is not good. He felt that the statement had to be moved forward and agreed to change the wording. He also said that the applicants would certainly be pleased to have an international dimension. On 21 October the Commission took stock of the hearings. The committee president, Mr Mihkelson, again proposed that the initiator withdraw the text and start drafting a new text. This was not accepted by the applicants. Ruuben Kaalep stressed that the statement needed to be discussed in the plenary session of the Riigikogu and that the statement should be adopted as such. Mr Mihkelson put the text of the statement to the vote in committee. The result of the vote was 2 in favour, 6 against and 2 abstentions. The Commission decided not to support the adoption of the statement in this form. Mikhail Lotman was appointed to present this statement, and I did it for him here today. I will add a few corrections to the previous statement by Reuben. The Tribal Peoples Programme, established by the Government of the Republic, is currently the fifth tribal programme in Estonia for the years 2021 to 2027. It is true that this concerns not all the indigenous peoples of Russia, but the Finno-Ugric people, including the Nenets and Uralic peoples. As regards the naming of genocide in this text, it is problematic for me too. From an international perspective, a consensus has been reached that genocide has occurred in very few cases. We all know these cases internationally. The deportation of Estonians in 1949, for example, may have elements of genocide, but so far it is not qualified for genocide. If we come up with such a statement and use the word 'genocide', I must agree with the experts who went to the committee to talk about it, that we are putting ourselves under a very simple blow, and the result may be that we are on our backs when we get news that the Estonian Riigikogu has wrongly accused the Russian Federation of the genocide of indigenous peoples. Every self-respecting expert in international law, even in Estonia, not to mention the Western countries, is forced to say that they believe that genocide does not take place, or at least they do not have any information about it. All members of the Committee on Foreign Affairs, I am sure that most members of the Riigikogu agree that our tribal peoples in the Russian Federation and indigenous peoples are discriminated against at all. We have no dispute on this issue; we all obviously agree that Estonia must do everything in its power to help them. Most members of the Committee on Foreign Affairs consider that adopting such a statement in this way would rather not help them and would rather reduce our chances of helping them believably and effectively in the future. I am very sorry that we did not reach an agreement between the political groups on the text of this statement. I would very much like us to come back to this issue, either in the Committee on Foreign Affairs or in another format, as soon as possible. But today, unfortunately, I have to recommend, at least for my part, not to accept the text of this statement. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U300	Mr President-in-Office of the Council. Ladies and gentlemen, The draft decision moved around here in the corridors of the Riigikogu in the spring of this year, because in June, the World Congress of the Finno-Ugric Peoples of VIII was coming in Estonia and the fate of Finno-Ugric peoples, the tribal companions of Russia in particular, was a particularly topical issue. I personally did not belong to the authors of the original text of this draft decision, but since the fate of the tribal brothers has been a subject close to my heart for many years, and since I thought it was important that this very important subject could be discussed by the Riigikogu both in the Committee on Foreign Affairs and in the large hall, I signed my support letter and this draft decision came into Riigikogu proceedings. Indeed, as has been rightly recalled here, we have the privilege of being Estonians. We are one of three Finno-Ugric people who have had history playing their part in creating a nation state in its full dimensions. Very many other Finno-Ugric peoples living in Russia have not had such an opportunity, at least so far. As Estonians, perhaps we have a moral obligation to contribute to our tribal brothers if they are to be persecuted in some way by Russian rulers. This is indeed our moral obligation to the Finno-Ugric world. Indeed, I am pleased that the Committee on Foreign Affairs took this issue seriously. It was not just the draft that was discussed among itself, but the experts involved in this discussion process, both human rights experts, representatives of cultural societies, and undoubtedly representatives of Fenno-Ugria, who really have perhaps the best expertise on this Finno-Ugric issue. During these in-depth discussions, as the good colleague Eerik-Niles pointed out, both substantive errors and a number of factual errors emerged in the text of this draft decision. ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U335	Let's go to the seventh item on today's agenda. To this end, the Government of the Republic has initiated a draft law amending the Law on Medicinal Products and related amendments to other laws 413. This is a first reading. First of all, the rapporteur is the Minister of Health and Labour, Tanel Kiik. Please, Tanel!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U336	Honourable Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu! Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I will introduce you to the draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and related amendments to other laws 413. This draft relates to Regulation No 536/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council on clinical trials on medicinal products for human use and repealing earlier Directive 2001/20/EC. The purpose of the directly applicable EU Regulation is to harmonise the requirements for clinical trials across the EU in order to ensure that the data obtained from clinical trials are stable and reliable, that patients have access to safer treatments, and that uniform requirements are in place to ensure the quality and safety of medicinal products used in clinical trials. At the same time, it also ensures a high level of protection of patients' rights and safety throughout the European Union. As a major change, the process of applying for the authorisation of clinical trials will clearly be simplified, i.e. once the new EU Regulation is in force, this process will be simplified so that the application for a study permit will be made on the basis of a single application in all the Member States requested, through the European Union Clinical Trials Portal and paper-free. This application for a clinical trial shall be assessed jointly by the Member States and a joint final decision shall be drawn up for that purpose. The final results of any clinical trial shall be in a common European Union database and shall therefore be made available to the public, such as a summary of the study assessment and the results of the survey inspection. The sponsor shall also pay a one-off fee in one Member State when applying for a clinical trial. At present, these fees exist in several countries, in many different ways. In Estonia, for example, there is a separate Medicines Agency [payable fee], an Ethics Committee [payable fee] and a state fee, so it also clearly simplifies. The applicant for a clinical trial for a medicinal product shall be able to communicate throughout the process with the Agency of one Member State, which shall coordinate the procedure and exchange of information between the applicant and the other Member States and which shall also prepare a scientific evaluation report on the application. The Estonian Medicines Agency, which has long experience in coordinating and evaluating international processes in assessing applications for marketing authorisations for medicinal products, is also ready to take this role. The Agency will also act as the Estonian contact point for clinical trials in the European Union. This means that the authorisation process is coordinated by the Agency, involving and taking into account the assessment made by the Ethics Committee. It also regulates the areas of clinical trials on medicinal products which are left to the discretion of the Member States under the EU Regulation. Such issues include evaluation of ethical aspects related to a clinical trial, determining the language of the application dossier, differences in obtaining informed consent from persons with reduced capacity, admission of medicinal products for use in a clinical trial to the territory of the country, rules governing the extent and assumptions of compensation for the harm caused to the subject, qualification requirements of the investigator, penalties for non-compliance, professional evaluation fees for the assessment of a clinical trial and other requirements for national regulation. Another important major package of amendments is that there is an opportunity to compensate for the damage caused by the clinical trial. We know that it is important for the patient to always ensure maximum safety, maximum security. And now, unlike the current system, according to the new regulation, the Estonian Health Insurance Fund will compensate for any health damage caused by taking part in a clinical trial of a medicinal product or medical device and using a hospital exemption medicine. In turn, the Health Insurance Fund will have a right of recovery against the sponsor of the clinical trial. The current regime does not provide for such an option. A third major change, to be mentioned separately, will be the possibility of preparing and using in hospitals, exceptionally, advanced medicines, or hospital exemption medicines. In addition to updating the requirements for pharmaceutical research, these changes will create the possibility of preparing and using, as indicated, novel medicines, or hospital exemptions, in hospitals. Article 3 (7) of Directive 2001/83/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council provides for the possibility, exceptionally, of preparing and using novelty medicinal products on a patient-specific basis under the sole professional responsibility of a specialised medical practitioner. These are medicines called advanced therapy medicinal products, which are divided into gene therapy, somatic cell therapy and tissue engineered medicines in the European Union, and which provide patients with new therapies for the treatment of certain types of diseases and dysfunctions. The hospital exemption is the exceptional preparation and use of a novelty medicine, in this case in Estonia, on the basis of an individual request from a specialist doctor and under professional responsibility for the treatment of a specific patient in a hospital. By way of derogation, the medicinal product may be prepared under conditions where the patient's current treatment options are exhausted, i.e. the use of the medicinal product is medically justified and there is sufficient scientific evidence to suggest that the benefit outweighs the potential risk of the medicinal product being used. This amendment allows patients to be offered treatment options that were not available to date. In addition, on the basis of data obtained from the use of a hospital dispenser, it is possible to start clinical trials with the medicinal product at a later stage and then to obtain a marketing authorisation, which is the usual situation for the medicinal products to be authorised accordingly. The regulation of the hospital exemption also allows planning of specific developments and opportunities for cooperation between Estonian universities, biotechnology companies and hospitals. There is a financial envelope for these costs arising from the amendments necessary for the implementation of the European Union Regulation. The Agency has been allocated a total of EUR 172644 from the state budget for implementation of the Regulation for the period 2022 to 2025, which is EUR 43161 per year. The Structural Funds also finance a one-off cost of EUR 267200 for the development of the Agency's clinical trial database. In view of the above, the draft has also proposed amendments to the Medical Device Act, to the Law on the procurement, handling and transplantation of cells, tissues and organs, to the Health Insurance Act, to the State Fees Act and to the Blood Act. The legislative amendment package is planned to enter into force on 31 January 2022, with amendments to the legislation on clinical trials of veterinary medicinal products coming into force on 28 January 2022, and the provisions on hospital exemption regulation will enter into force in general. It's quite technical, but it's an important bill for the pharmaceutical sector. That concludes the introduction and I am happy to answer the questions. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U337	There are some questions. Kalle Grünthal, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U338	Thank you, Mr President. Honourable waitress! This law also deals with gene therapy and mRNA therapy. ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U343	Kert Kingo, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U344	Thank you, Mr President. Honourable waitress! Does this regulation fix the need for prior consent of the patient to conduct a clinical trial on these new medicinal products, or may it be done without the consent of the patient?
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U345	Thank you. When we talk about the general principle, of course participation in pharmaceutical studies, participation in clinical trials always requires the consent of the patient. Rather, the logic of the draft is, as I have pointed out, to reduce the bureaucratic side at the expense of the study that is being carried out and on the basis of which any specific authorisation is sought, not to apply separately in several dozen points, which extend the whole process, but to launch it on the basis of a single application for authorisation in several Member States of the European Union. The countries of the European Union communicate with each other through pharmaceutical agencies in order to agree on these requirements, criteria according to the procedures in force. As I pointed out, the golden rule is to get the patient's consent. Among other things, as I have pointed out, the right to reimbursement will be added as a positive change for the patient should there be any health damage arising from the use of a hospital dispenser or from participating in a clinical trial, in contrast to the previous one. So in this light, the situation is actually getting more favourable for the patient. If you are asking whether a hospital dispenser can be used under any circumstances without the patient's consent, you really need to specify this, because the hospital dispenser can be used in a very difficult situation where, for example, family members or other close relatives decide whether to use this particular treatment. As I have pointed out, this concerns patients whose other treatment options have been exhausted. In essence, this is an extremely critical situation where the patient may no longer be able to give consent or refuse to do so. In such a situation, a distinction may be allowed as to whether the authorisation is granted by close relatives.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U346	Aivar Chef, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U347	Good manager! Dear Minister, Let's move on from here to the question of the previous speaker. It is still very clear here: "The legal framework for the hospital exemption allows researchers and smaller pharmaceutical companies to create innovative medicines for specific patients and at the same time ensures their safety and the expected effectiveness. It was also answered that the draft concerns the procedure for clinical trials and that if the pharmaceutical manufacturer chooses Estonia as the country of destination, it may also concern any medicinal products, including COVID-19 medicinal products. The Commission was also interested in the decision-making process for the pricing and introduction of advanced therapy medicinal products by hospital exemption. This was answered by the fact that the hospital exception can work in a situation where all authorised medicines have been used and none has been effective. In such a case, the research organisation or organiser will pay for the treatment, so that there will be no additional costs for the patient or the Estonian Health Insurance Fund. The Commission also examined how the methodology for the payment of the fee would change. It was answered that, for the European Commission, reducing red tape is an important objective of the regulation. According to the current system, the conductor of a clinical trial must pay a separate fee to both the Agency and the Ethics Committee, but according to the new regulation, the investigator only pays a fee to the Agency, which, according to the Partner Agreement, pays the work carried out by the Ethics Committee itself. In addition, Estonia's cost-based system has been compared to that of neighbouring countries and it has been found that our fees are significantly more favourable, which means that the amount of the fee could rather attract clinical trials of medicines to Estonia. At the same time, Estonia's small population does not support research. The Commission took the following decisions: to propose a draft to be put on the plenary agenda today, 27 October, the decision was consensual; to propose the completion of the first reading, to be consensual; and, thirdly, to appoint Siret Kotka as the representative of the lead committee. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U373	Today's eighth item: First reading of the draft law amending the Health Insurance Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. The rapporteur is Mr Tanel Kiik, Minister for Health and Labour. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U374	Honourable Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu! Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I now introduce to you draft law amending the Health Insurance Act 467. The aim of this draft is to improve access to dental care for the less-insured adult population. The most important objective of dental care benefits is to reduce health inequalities by also improving access to dental care for those who may otherwise be unable to obtain it for financial reasons. Currently, two compensation mechanisms are known to be in place. One is a 40-euro dental benefit for adults, and the other is at a higher rate, i.e. a 85-euro dental benefit with an own contribution of 15% for pensioners over 63 years of age, incapacity pensioners, old-age pensioners, partially or partially disabled persons, pregnant women, mothers of children under one year of age, as well as those who have an increased need for dental care due to their medical condition. According to this draft, we will extend the target group of persons receiving dental benefits at an increased threshold. This includes people who have registered in the unemployment fund as unemployed for the purposes of the Employment Services and Benefits Act. It's estimated at 60,000 now, but that number's gonna change. There have been times when he has been over 100,000, and there have been times when he has been considerably smaller. And secondly, the target group will be added to people who receive income support under the Social Welfare Act. More specifically, it is assessed whether the person has received income support during the two calendar months preceding the month in which he or she received dental care. For these, the target population is up to 1300 people. In fact, the range of beneficiaries of income support is, of course, wider, but as many of them overlap with those who are already unemployed, the other half does not increase so much [the beneficiaries]. The benefit at the increased threshold extends to the whole calendar year, regardless of whether or not a person will receive income support in the future. Under the Labour Market Services and Benefits Act, data on unemployed persons are already available in the Health Insurance Fund's database, according to the Health Insurance Act. The Social Insurance Board will transmit the data to the Health Insurance Fund according to the Health Insurance Act and the Basic Regulation of the Social Services and Benefits Data Register. The data exchange takes place through the X-Road of data exchange every time you inherit dental care, which means that the system is relatively patient-friendly. The proposed amendments were approved by the Government Office on 17 June 2021. Later, we also tabled a corresponding bill, for which we certainly hope the support of Parliament as well. The additional cost of implementing the proposed changes will be estimated at EUR 1 million for the Health Insurance Fund next year, and in future years, depending, of course, on the unemployment rate. These costs have now been taken into account in the preparation of the Health Insurance Fund's budget. The proposed date of entry into force of the Act is 1 January 2022. The background is so much that we certainly want to move forward in the future with increased dental benefits and a certain degree of flexibility. Ensuring access to dental care is, of course, one of the places where the Estonian health care system needs extra money, or rather, Estonian patients need extra money, and Estonian people receive extra money. I very much hope that in the coming years it will also be possible to find more of this through budget requests so that the dental health of Estonian people improves. Thank you.

ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U384	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! The fundamental value of Estonian health insurance, the main objective of solidarity, is to ensure that people have access to the necessary health services regardless of their economic situation and the size of their income. The analysis of the World Health Organization's own contribution policy shows that, despite the fundamental values of health insurance, the cost of treatment paid by Estonian people is high and the risk of poverty due to high medical costs is also very high. Such inequalities in health and the use of healthcare due to the limited financial income of people are unfair. The role of the state is to look through policies for ways of reducing people's own participation in health care and protecting people from high costs and poverty. I am pleased that this draft extension of the increased threshold for dental benefits allows this. Oral health care is very important. It is part of the overall health condition of a person and we know that oral health can also affect the functioning of other organ systems. Oral diseases can cause both local and aesthetic problems, which in turn affect a person's quality of life. According to a study carried out by Kantar Emor in 2020, 58% of the population aged 15 years and older attended dental examinations during the last 12 months. This is 6% less than in 2018. Although the main reason for not attending the dentist's office was the mention of a good enough condition for the teeth, 38% of it was said, economic reasons continue to play an important role. For 32% of respondents, the economic situation is the main reason for less than once a year visits to dentists. According to the survey, the average number of people aged 35 to 49 with lower incomes (the lower income is those who receive up to EUR 500 per family member per month) and those who have children under 18 years of age are not visited by a dentist for economic reasons. We know that from 1 January 2018, all adult insured persons receive EUR 40 per year of dental care benefit. The benefit applies to essential dental care services and can be used in dental care establishments that have concluded a contract with a health insurance fund throughout Estonia. The patient's own contribution for the compensation of EUR 40 is 50%. Today it is possible to receive a higher rate of 85 euros per year in dental care benefits for pensioners over 63 years of age, disability pensioners, old-age pensioners, people with partial or lack of working capacity, pregnant women, mothers of children under one year of age, and people who have an increased need for dental care due to their medical condition. It is gratifying that, on 1 January 2022, these target groups will also be accompanied by registered unemployed persons and beneficiaries of income support, which are estimated to be around 55000 people. This gives these people the possibility to use dental benefits of up to EUR 85 per year with a mandatory own contribution of up to 15%, i.e. EUR 15. This change will help to improve access to dental care for the less-insured adult population. Based on various studies, low-income households and unemployed are precisely those groups that are currently... I'd like a few more minutes. Thank you.
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U385	Three more minutes, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2021-10-27_U386	...for high costs, leaving dental care out of service. Unfortunately, as a result, the oral health and hygiene of these target groups is worse, which could be improved by dental visits. It is worth noting that if a person belongs to a target group of beneficiaries of an increased level of benefit, he or she will receive an increased level of benefit during the calendar year, regardless of whether he or she will receive income support in the future. Looking ahead, the priority is to look for ways to protect the population even more effectively against high medical costs and to make dental care more accessible in a targeted way to those for whom it is currently not available for financial reasons.
